Bitcoin Slides to $73,510 Over the Weekend: Did Nvidia Scandal Trigger BTC’s $2,000 Flash Crash?

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin news: BTC fell to $73,510 on Sunday, 100 minutes after the Nvidia scandal broke. Thin weekend trading let the drop run further.

In Bitcoin news overnight, BTC USD price fell from $75,607 to $73,510 in ten minutes on Sunday evening, its lowest price since August 21. The worst of it came at 21:55 UTC, or 4:55 p.m. in New York. In that single minute, Bitcoin dropped 1.9% on Binance.

Most traders blamed Nvidia. Earlier that afternoon, CNBC reported that Nvidia’s board had opened an independent investigation into CEO Jensen Huang’s relationship with a woman U.S. intelligence agencies believe was passing information to Beijing (it’s just speculation for now).

Huang admitted the affair in an email to staff.

After Jensen Huang admitted having an affair, a whale opened a 10x short on 30,000 $NVDA ($5.44M) on Hyperliquid.https://t.co/RztJx5FNge pic.twitter.com/zuyV2K0ftN

— Lookonchain (@lookonchain) November 9, 2026

The bounce since then has been small. BTC traded at $74,184 on Binance on Monday morning, 2.2% below where it stood when the Nvidia story broke. The drop also marked a fifth straight losing week: the weekly candle closed at $74,851, below the week’s open of $78,354.

So did the Nvidia scandal really trigger the dump, or did thin weekend trading turn an ordinary sell-off into a sharp one?

Discover: Sam Altman ChatGPT AI Predicts an Insane Move for Cardano (ADA)

The timing does not fit a straight reaction to the news. CNBC published its report at 3:12 p.m. Eastern time on Sunday, which is 20:12 UTC. Bitcoin traded at $75,880 on Binance in that minute. For the next 90 minutes, almost nothing happened. At 21:44 UTC, BTC sat at $75,607, just 0.4% lower than when the story appeared.

Then the selling started, and it started in Ethereum. Between 21:50 and 21:55 UTC, ETH fell 6.9% on Binance, from $2,213 to $2,060. Bitcoin fell 2.4% over the same five minutes.

That pattern points to a liquidation cascade, a chain of forced sales. Traders who borrow to bet on higher prices get their positions force-closed when the price falls past a set point. Each forced sale pushes the price down to the next group of bets.

BREAKING: $1,000,000,000 liquidated from the crypto market in the past 10 minutes.$BTC falls under $74,000$ETH falls under $2,125$XRP falls under $1.10 $SOL falls under $90 pic.twitter.com/hIZhzQFJJe

— Watcher.Guru (@WatcherGuru) November 8, 2026

The news still mattered. Nvidia is the biggest AI stock, and Bitcoin has traded in step with AI shares for weeks: Thursday’s chip-stock sell-off is what pushed BTC below its 200-day average. With stock markets closed, crypto was the only market open to sell into the headline.

A second headline added to the Bitcoin news mood. Secretary of State Marco Rubio called Iran’s demands “outrageous” and said talks had made no progress.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

Weekend trading explains why the drop went so far. Over the past year, about $24 million of BTC changed hands on Binance in a typical weekend hour, half the $48 million in a typical weekday hour.

We checked every hour in that year in which Bitcoin fell at least 2.5% below the hour’s opening price. There were 29 of them.

Sunday’s drop came with $180 million of trading in the hour. That is the second-lowest of all 29, and about half the median of $366 million.

In other words, it took less selling than usual to move the price this far. The busiest minute, 21:55 UTC, traded $75 million of BTC on Binance, about three times what changes hands in a whole weekend hour.

Weekends do not cause more crashes, though. Only 6 of the 29 drops came on a Saturday or Sunday, slightly fewer than the 29% of all hours that fall on weekends. What a quiet weekend changes is how far the price moves for each dollar sold.

One note: much of the forced selling happens in futures markets, which this count does not include.

So both played a part. Nvidia and Iran gave traders a reason to cut risk, and thin weekend trading let the selling run further than it would on a weekday.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best New Crypto to Hodl

It held on the first test. BTC bounced to $75,735 within two hours of the low, but it has slipped back to $74,184, and the daily chart still leans lower.

Bitcoin trades below all three of its daily exponential moving averages (EMAs), trend lines that give recent prices more weight. It lost the 200-day EMA, which tracks the long-term trend, on Thursday.

For traders following Bitcoin news this week, Monday’s stock market open is the next test. It is the first chance for Nvidia shares to trade on the news, eight days before the company reports earnings on November 17.

Sheffield United rival Portsmouth in race for star who can produce 'absolutely crazy numbers'

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sheffield United are already looking ahead to the January transfer window, as Chris Wilder prepares to strengthen areas of weakness throughout the Blades squad, though they may well face some strong competition from division rivals Portsmouth.

Sheffield United are looking forward to the January transfer window and have set their sights on Belgian youth international Renzo Tytens to reinforce their defensive options.

However, Championship rivals Portsmouth are also heavy admirers of the current Lyngby left-back, as this saga is set to heat up as the winter window draws ever closer.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top nine toughest fanbases in the EFL Championship right now from least to most

According to Rudy Galetti's Substack, the Belgian journalist has reported that Portsmouth are the current frontrunners for the signature of Tytens, though they face a threat from Sheffield United and French side Toulouse.

Tytens, 21, came through the youth ranks of Dutch outfit PSV Eindhoven before spending a brief period with Club Brugge's youth team between the summer of 2024 and January 2026, when he moved to Denmark with Lyngby.

Since joining Lyngby, he has helped his side achieve promotion back to the top flight and locked down the left-back spot as his own, with his performances in the Superligaen attracting interest across Europe.

Speaking to Danish publication Tipsbladet, former Brentford defender and current Lyngby coach, Andreas Bjelland, was full of praise for Tytens, as he said: "Our scouting department and sports director have done a fantastic job finding Renzo, who we think is a good fit. He's not Gustav Mortensen, but he's a bit like that.

"He can produce some absolutely crazy numbers and has a good foot and has proven to be really, really important for us already."

Sheffield United are also reportedly considering Motherwell's Emmanuel Longelo, though no formal discussions have taken place at this stage for either player.

Sheffield United's interest in Tytens and Longelo will surely come as a surprise to many, with each of Harrison Burrows, Sam McCallum, and Rhys Norrington-Davies already battling for a starting berth at left-back at Bramall Lane.

However, Wilder may well be looking to the future, and with Norrington-Davies out of contract next summer, a gap in the Blades' squad may open up for Tytens, though surely one of Burrows or McCallum must leave also.

Portsmouth, meanwhile, have Jayden Meghoma and Connor Ogilvie battling for a place in the Pompey side under John Mousinho, though with Meghoma only on loan from Brentford and Ogilvie now 30, it's hardly a shock the South Coast outfit may be looking to the future with Tytens.

Portsmouth are the favourites for his signature as things stand, and having spent time in two prestigious academies and gaining experience in a top-flight European league in three different countries already, Tytens could well be making England his next destination sooner rather than later.

What do you make of this potential addition, Portsmouth and Sheffield United fans? Would you like to see Tytens head to your club in January, or should you be looking elsewhere? Share your thoughts below!

Now into the midst of a two-week international break, clubs across the Championship will take a step back to assess their current squads and begin making preparations for the January window, as Sheffield United and Portsmouth will be no different.

Both sit just outside of the play-off positions as things stand, and both Wilder and Mousinho will feel as though there is no reason their sides can't go on and claim one of those top eight spots.

The winter window will provide a chance for both to strengthen areas of weakness, as a move for Tytens appears to be on the cards for both.

Toncoin (TON), now rebranded as Gram (GRAM), has entered a new phase after an 81.22% community vote approved the name change in June 2026.

As the native token of The Open Network, Gram continues to power Telegram’s growing Web3 ecosystem, giving it access to one of the largest user bases in crypto. With Telegram expanding blockchain features, rising ecosystem activity, and improving market sentiment, investors are closely watching whether GRAM can build long-term momentum.

But can it reclaim its previous highs and deliver strong returns in the coming years?

Let’s dive into CoinPedia’s Gram (GRAM) price prediction for 2026–2030 to explore its future potential.

Gram (formerly Toncoin) is down 6.02% to $0.998 in the past 24 hours, falling more than the broader crypto market. The main reason is a break below the important $1 support level, which has increased selling pressure.

At the same time, its 24-hour trading volume jumped 53% to $71.15 million, showing that more traders were selling. The drop may have also triggered stop-loss orders and added to selling pressure.

Overall, the decline appears to be driven by weak market conditions and technical factors, rather than any major news about the project.

GRAM’s short-term outlook remains weak as long as it trades below $1. If selling continues, the next major support level is around $0.95.

GRAM’s growth in 2026 will mainly depend on Telegram’s expanding blockchain ecosystem and rising real-world use of the token.

The network also benefits from its token model. A small portion of every transaction fee is permanently burned, gradually reducing the circulating supply. At the same time, TON Strategy has announced a $250 million investment plan to build its long-term GRAM treasury, providing additional market support.

Telegram is also improving network efficiency by lowering transaction fees, making payments cheaper and encouraging wider adoption across mini apps, payments, and digital services.

The immediate resistance is at $1.559. A strong daily close above this level would strengthen the W-pattern setup and could push GRAM toward the next major resistance at $2.3478.

On the downside, $1.407 is the key support shown on the chart. Holding this level would keep the recent recovery structure intact, while a daily break below it could send GRAM back toward the 1.30–1.35 accumulation zone.

AI Slop FM radio experiment puts machines on trial, one dodgy tune at a time

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Music fan from Wales lets listeners pit AI-generated songs against human bops – including toilet operas and HR protest songs

A Welsh music enthusiast has built an internet radio station that plays AI-generated tunes alongside human-made tracks without telling listeners which is which, to find out whether listeners really hate AI music or just the idea of it.

AI Slop FM is the creation of Mike Chaffin, who built and runs the station single-handedly from Ammanford, Carmarthenshire. By stripping away artist names, cover art, and any mention of AI, Chaffin hopes to find out what listeners really think when they have nothing but the music to go on.

The free 24/7 station asks listeners to rate tracks from S to E, tag how the music makes them feel, and leave written feedback. The most popular submissions will eventually make it into a weekly chart called the Slop 40, giving creators something that can be hard to come by in the age of streaming: an actual audience.

Anyone can submit a track, including human musicians, although Chaffin has already discovered that not all of them are particularly keen on the idea. He told The Register that he had floated the idea to an unsigned musician friend who had sold around 600 albums, but was met with a rather nervous response.

"Wh, wh, why would I want to do that?" the musician reportedly asked, according to Chaffin, who said he had never heard his friend stutter before.

"It seems that there is a deep rooted fear of AI music in the industry," Chaffin said.

The station is still in beta, with Chaffin waiting for enough submissions to fill its schedule across different genres before opening it fully. He's already been pleasantly surprised by the quality of the music submitted, although he still listens to every track to make sure nobody is trying to pass off Bohemian Rhapsody as their own work.

Among the early offerings are what Chaffin describes as a developing genre of slightly rude protest songs about HR departments, along with an operatic epic about a blocked toilet.

"Some of which are hilarious," he said of the HR songs. As for the toilet opera, it "runs through my head about once a day, whether I like it or not."

Chaffin believes the sheer volume of music uploaded to streaming platforms means plenty of perfectly decent tracks never find an audience, whether a person or a machine made them. He hopes AI Slop FM will give some of those overlooked creations a chance to be heard.

"You don't have to have published your creations to upload. Just to own the rights," he explained.

Tracks that don't make the Slop 40 could still get another outing, with plans for genre-specific stations offering unsuccessful submissions a second chance.

The bigger question is what happens when listeners start rating AI-generated music more highly than the human competition, but Chaffin isn't particularly worried about that.

"If the machines win then their creators clearly deserve to be heard, deserve an audience. If the musicians win, then that's reassuring for them."

He also wants to see how the station's eventual top five or top ten compares with the official music charts, although with submissions having only recently opened, there are no results to pore over just yet.

"Everyone has an opinion on music before a note has been heard, by the genre, the label, the name or the cover art," Chaffin said. "We strip all that away, tracks are judged purely on the music."

Whether listeners ultimately prefer human musicians or their algorithmically assisted rivals remains to be seen. Either way, somewhere in Carmarthenshire, an opera about a blocked toilet is proving rather difficult to flush from its listener's memory. ®

La gara è in programma sabato 21 novembre alle 15:00; per gli acquisti online è prevista una commissione del 3,80%.

I biglietti per Lazio-Lecce saranno in vendita dalle 11:00 di lunedì 9 novembre. La partita di Serie A Enilive è in programma sabato 21 novembre alle 15:00. Secondo quanto riportato da Sslazio, La S.S. Lazio ha comunicato le modalità di acquisto, le agevolazioni e le indicazioni per l’accesso allo Stadio Olimpico.

Chi possiede una fidelity card Millenovecento o Eagle e non è abbonato può chiedere, anche acquistando online, che il titolo d’ingresso venga caricato elettronicamente sulla carta. Ai biglietti comprati online si applica una commissione di servizio del 3,80%.

Nella vendita libera una persona può acquistare al massimo quattro biglietti, secondo le disposizioni dell’Osservatorio sulle Manifestazioni Sportive. Per gli altri tre eventuali spettatori è sufficiente presentare anche una copia del documento d’identità.

Il biglietto Ridotto Under 16 è riservato ai ragazzi nati dal 1° gennaio 2010. Per i nati dal 1° gennaio 2012 è prevista l’iniziativa Omaggio Under 14: possono entrare gratuitamente se accompagnati da un genitore, o da un parente entro il quarto grado, titolare di un biglietto a pagamento.

Il tagliando omaggio, nominativo, va ritirato insieme all’acquisto del biglietto dell’accompagnatore. L’operazione si svolge esclusivamente nei Lazio Style 1900, tranne Parco Leonardo, oppure, solo il giorno della gara, alla biglietteria di Via Nigra. I bambini nati dal 1° gennaio 2022, indicati nella comunicazione come bambini di quattro anni, entrano gratuitamente senza biglietto presentando un documento d’identità o la tessera sanitaria. All’ingresso l’identità sarà controllata per ogni spettatore: anche i minorenni devono esibire un documento.

Fun things to do with your VM/370 CE machine – Fun with big computers

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This page assumes you have a machine (emulated or not) running the VM370 Community Edition OS available. There are a couple ways to get one, and you might want to choose the one that appeals to you the most (or the one that’s less work - it’s up to you).

The Community Edition is a huge set of improvements and fixes applied on top of the last VM/370 that was released by IBM without a license restricting its use. These improvements and fixes have been developed and published in a variety of ways since the release of the original OS. If we were to compare it with Linux, VM/370 R6 (the release CE is based on) would be Linux 1.0 as published by Linus from his bedroom and VM/370 CE would be latest Fedora (insert your favorite maximalist distro here). It has many features that didn’t exist in R6 and a couple things that would have been called science fiction back then. It’s quite capable, even if the machines it can run on are limited to a single CPU and 16M of memory (IBM calls it “storage”).

The easiest way to run this is with Docker installed. How to install it depends on your platform, and we won’t cover it here. Besides, Docker is only one option - you can use Podman as well - it has some nice features, and can pretend it’s Docker, so you can use the same commands with both. When you are set up, you can just issue a shell command to have your own machine.

This will bring up the machine and display the machine’s console on your terminal.

The container image we are using is based on the excellent work of the VM/370 Community Edition team. You can see their releases on vm370.org. The Community Editions runs only software that’s either open source or public domain.

If you work in an organization that’s cool enough to have an actual IBM mainframe, you can check if your operations team will allow you to have a small VM running nested VM/370 Community Edition. IBM mainframes have legendary backwards compatibility and there should be no (technical) issue bringing up an environment for you. There might be a lot of process hurdles, however, so tread carefully and have a good excuse at hand. “Doing fun stuff” is not it. Trust me, I’ve tried this approach and it didn’t end well.

Another possible source of used mainframes is eBay or your local IT asset disposal company, but, seriously, you probably don’t want the hassle of a dedicated power line for the machine, or the basement space to have one. And, if you get a real machine, you might also get a valid license to run something more modern, like z/VM, z/OS, and a fairly modern Linux. They are also quite expensive and require expertise to set them up, and, many times, to fix them (people who decommission these machines often don’t bother disconnecting cables when just cutting is faster).

IBM mainframes use screen-oriented terminals. They worked more or less like a web browser - the mainframe would send you a screen with a form, you’d write some information on the fields of the form, and the terminal would send the information back to the mainframe, restarting the cycle. If you think this looks like how HTML forms work, you are right, but this was in the late 1960s, not the mid 1990s.

Our examples use the x3270 emulator that’s available on any modern Unix-like environment (which includes Linux).

Start x3270 and connect it to your host. If you are running on your computer, it’ll be “localhost:3270”. Since I’m running mine on a small cluster, I’ll point the terminal to the entry address in the cluster.

With that taken out of our way, we can start to have fun.

For normal things, we’ll log on to the CMSUSER VM, or, if you renamed it, to your own VM. The default password is “CMSUSER”:

The screen will clear. You can then enter logon cmsuser:

As expected, the password isn’t shown. The red cursor means that.

You can also logon using LOGON CMSUSER CMSUSER, typing the password after the user name, but that horrifies me even more than having the username as the password. Back when VM/370 was invented, intrusions were a lot less of a problem than they are now. Please do not expose your VM/370 mainframe directly on the internet.

At this point, you are logged on. There is a lot going on under the hood - CP, or “control program” asked VM/370 to create a virtual machine for you, and this virtual machine was started with the CMS operating system. The commands we’ll see are CMS commands (unless noted). When you press ENTER again, a program, called “PROFILE EXEC” will run:

We can examine it. Note that “PROFILE EXEC” means the name is “PROFILE” and the type is “EXEC” (a script). To show the contents of the file, use:

Unlike Unix, commands and file names are case insensitive, so it’s fine to type them either way. Be careful, however, so you don’t overwrite your files accidentally.

Unlike most terminals, a 3270 doesn’t scroll. If you fill the screen, you’ll need to press the PA2 key (don’t look for one on your keyboard - there isn’t one). PA2 clears the terminal and receives the next page of text from the mainframe. On x3270, PA2 is Alt-2. On c3270, it’s Control-C.

Our current PROFILE EXEC prints a welcome message that’s informative, but that we can always read later. Let’s remove that message from our profile. For that, we’ll use a little bit of the EDIT editor.

Now we need to move down 12 lines. The command space is in the bottom of the screen, with the green cursor. Enter DOWN 12. This will move the highlight to the line we want to delete. Now use the DELETE command.

Now, to save the file and exit, enter FILE. You’ll see the MORE prompt in the bottom right - this indicates you need to press the PA2 key (Alt-2) to continue. 3270 terminals are screen oriented (think web browsers in the 1960s) and don’t scroll - you need to ask them for the next page. This has the advantage of allowing you to edit information on your screen and only causing the computer (the mainframe, in our case) to pay attention when you send it the information you edited. Most other computers that use terminals have to deal with each keystroke separately.

With this change done, the welcome message will not be displayed when you log in. If you want to see it again, you’ll need to type TYPE WELCOME MESSAGE U. Try it.

What is a vintage computer without a BASIC interpreter, right?

Let’s start with the most basic BASIC program: a Hello World.

Note that “basic” is not a suffix, but a type - it’s a separate parameter. The type of the file is a property of the file and will remain with it unless forcibly changed.

When you press enter (or send) you’ll end up on the EDIT program. On the surface, it looks a lot like vi from the Unix side: it’s terse, hard to use, and sometimes counter-intuitive. It’s also hard to guess how to exit it. That’s about where the similarities end.

When you press enter, you’ll see the line was entered. It was also changed to uppercase (see the “CASE=U” at the top line?). Let’s save the file and quit the editor. Type “file” and press Enter.

The line at the bottom shows a “MORE…” note. This means the terminal is waiting for you before it shows more output. Press PA2 (Alt-2 for x3270) and you’ll see you have exited EDIT.

You’ll see your command (“basic hello”) show up at the top of the screen (after the message indicating EDIT finished), then the message you ordered it to print “HELLO WORLD”, followed by another line telling how much CPU it used and how long did it take to run your program.

VM/370 CE comes with a couple hello world programs. You can use the DIR command (which is also something added to vanilla VM) to list what files are in the disk you are seeing. We typed our own in BASIC, but there is one in C and one in FORTRAN. Let’s see the FORTRAN one:

It’s about time we mention the message. It’s telling me the program run using 0.84 seconds of CPU and took 2.59 seconds to complete. It also told me what time it is for the computer.

After compiling the program, you might have noticed two new files appeared:

The first one is a nicely formatted listing, and, in ancient times, you’d find it printed alongside the punched cards you submitted for compilation. It is formatted for 132 columns, as big printers of the time used to be.

The other is telling me it’s a text, but it appears it’s a lie:

In order to run the Fortran program we just compiled, we’ll do:

So… LOAD will load one or more relocatable object code, which is the TEXT file (we knew it was a lie!). So, we are asking LOAD to load the HELLO TEXT file, and we are passing the START option to it (so the code runs).

Looks like nothing happened. But it actually did. There is a new file:

So, the program ran, and the output went into this oddly named file. This is the first parameter of the WRITE statement. WRITE(6,1) means write to file 6 using the format defined on the line labeled 1. You can also see the “6” in the middle of the file type. We can redirect the output of file 6 to the terminal:

Now the program outputs to the terminal. Why the first character is missing is an interesting problem. I am not sure why this is happening, neither are my current mainframe gurus. Our current best guess is that this is because the first character of a line being interpreted by the line printer. This is arcane knowledge I don’t fully understand myself, The “right” way to output to a terminal is, and I’ve seen it done in period source code, have a space in the first column of the output string. When the system is outputting to a file, it deals with the output and the first character is suppressed, as it would be by the printer.

What was the magic we used to redirect? Let’s see what FILEDEF does. Do a HELP FILEDEF:

… and so on. Fortran, by default, outputs to whatever is defined as file “6”, which ends up being “FILE FT06F001”. This is all related to the way batch processes were set up where things like input and outputs were specified by a configuration for when a program is run. This was done in a language called JCL (for Job Control Language). It was uncommon for programs to be run interactively at the terminal, and even having a terminal was considered a luxury most programmers didn’t have - they’d submit their code in special forms that someone else would type, convert to punch cards, and feed to the compiler.

There is a lot of code you can use. For exaple, the IBM System/360 Operating System FORTRAN IV Programmer’s Guide has an example, primes.fortran I copied into this repo:

When you upload it, make sure it’s fixed length at 80 characters, as punched cards were. If you forget, you’ll get a helpful error message:

You’ll now see that the file is now of fixed 80 character width (F 80 in the format column).

Again we see some oddness at the start of the output.

… but we can see the program completed successfully.

PL/I is another language popular in mainframes, and is used to this day. It’s much closer to more to what users of other recent languages would find familiar. It even includes a C-like preprocessor that allows including external files, conditional compilation and code-generating loops.

This will compile all the files mentioned. The compiler has lots of options, so it’s a good idea to read the built-in for the compiler. For that, as usual, type:

Rexx is the “glue language” of IBM mainframes. It’s simple, easy to use and interpreted. To write a REXX program you use the text editor. Using the file type EXEC will make it callable from the command line, but, in order for it to be recognized as a REXX program, the first line needs to be a comment delimited by /* and */. The language itself reminds me of later versions of BASIC after line numbers were no longer a thing:

This is interesting - there is a VM without a login that has the name ALGOL. [TBA]

The VM370CE distribution has a lot of disks. You can check which ones you have immediate access with. If you logged on as CMSUSER, you can use the LISTFILE (we abreviated it to “LIST”) command to see what files are on your default disk. In CMS, commands can be abbreviated - the actual command is LISTFILE but LIST is neat enough.

To check the disks you have, use QUERY DISK (query can also be abbreviated - Q will suffice for the computer to understand what you want):

To list what’s on a disk, you’ll use LISTFILE * * [disk]. The first “*” is a wildcard that will show all file names, the second is for all types. If you are familiar with windcards from CP/M, MS-DOS, or Unix, these don’t behave the same.

So… Looking at the output of LISTFILE and LISTFILE * * A, we can conclude we were start from disk A. In this case, A is a shortcut to disk 191 (mainframes always were supposed to have lots of disks, and our emulated one - a maxed out 4381 - has more than most companies could afford, or that would fit in most period accurate computer rooms). In our case, 191 is the “address” of the disk, and tells where the disk is connected to the virtual machine. Disks also have 6-character labels (or “VOLSER” in mainframe slang). A is “CMS190”.

Disks D, E, F, and G are empty. That wouldn’t be a surprise if we had paid attention to the output of QUERY DISK - it clearly says they have zero files, and use 5 blocks (rounded to 0% of the disk).

There is also disk S (or 190, or ). It has 172 files, so it’ll take a couple pages to list everything. In the list you’ll see a lot of interesting files - this disk contains system utilities - there’s ACCESS, EDIT, QUERY, SET, SORT, GLOBAL, and other things that we have seen before or that look like things an OS would provide (not a big surprise, as its label is “CMSDSK”). Binary programs seem to be of type “MODULE”. Let’s remember that. Try listing all files of type MODULE in S:

CMS offers a flat file system, so we can’t have subdirectories. This is a big difference for people who are used to Unix or Windows systems and to organize files using them. Another difference, that’ll feel more familiar to Windows users (and CP/M users before them) is that the system exposes multiple volumes instead of the single-root hierarchy of Unix systens.

Finally, a more subtle difference is that there is no such thing as the “current” disk. If you don’t specify a disk letter, the file is interpreted as A, which is your “home”. You don’t ever “change” to another disk, and you always mention them explicitly.

A plain VM/370 would map CMS system files to disk S. The Community Edition we are running has the OS on drive S, and OS extras on disk Y. Disk U contains utilities.

For this example, we’ll use the GAMES.VMARC file available at the h390-vm group at groups.io. The easiest way to upload a file to your mainframe is with the x3270 app - on the File menu select “File Transfer”:

Since we are uploading a VMARC file (think a .zip or .tar.bz2), we’ll chose a binary transfer. The file will be saved to our disk D.

It might be a while if you are running Hercules on an underpowered Raspberry Pi Zero W. I assume a local instance on a sensible computer will be a lot faster. When the transfer is finished, a new dialog will pop up telling you so.

Now we need to understand how to open a VMARC file. Luckily for us, VM370CE has a very comprehensive help system. On the terminal, issue a:

Listing the contents of the file seems like a good first step:

As most people know, the only winning move with Tic Tac Toe (and Global Thermonuclear War) is not to play. The other games are a lot more interesting.

Other two game archives that are easy to obtain are under the VM-370-Games repo on Github. Once you download the files to your desktop and sent them to your VM/370 machine, you’ll have:

The first one, VM86F155, has a lot of games in BASIC you can explore and modify (it you figure out how EDIT works).

The ZORK code is in FORTRAN, and, while there is a program called GENZORK, it fails (maybe it needs to run on a disk at B).

We are done with CMSUSER for now, so we’ll be good users and log off our machine:

It’s no fun to log in as CMSUSER every time you want to do something. You’ll want a user for yourself, with reasonable permissions to do things to the machine. In my case, I want to create an “RBANFFY” user for me.

Here’s the first surprise for those who come from other platforms - you wouldn’t create a user, you’d create a VM, and you log in to that VM. You can create a user without creating a VM for it, but that would not be what you actually want. Users and VMs are defined the same way, with the USER keyword.

Creating a VM is a bit involved - you’ll need to do a lot of things before - allocate and format storage, for instance. We’ll leave that for later.

In the meantime, if you want to rename the CMSUSER VM to your preferred name (and change the password), we can explain that. We’ll start logging on as MAINT (which means, logging on to the MAINT VM).

We’ll also learn a lot of interesting things about our system, and a couple new commands for the EDIT editor.

The first thing we want to do is to check which disks are available to this VM.

Note that we share a couple disks with other VMs. This is counterintuitive to people used to do VMs the Windows or Linux way - under VM/370 (and its descendants) VMs can share filesystems, not disks. Think of it as mounting a remote filesystem, but there is no remote server - this is done within the hypervisor. We are sharing volumes CMSDSK, MNT29D and MNT19E with other users (we saw it logged in as CMSUSER).

The file we want to look into is USER DIRECT. Let’s first find where it is located. Use LISTFILE USER DIRECT * to list files named USER DIRECT in all volumes you can access. Your terminal will show something like:

So, we see the file is in our default disk (A), which is volume MNT191. Let’s edit it - on your terminal, run EDIT USER DIRECT.

The top line tells you the file and the mode you are working in.

This tells me we have the USER DIRECT file, located on A (ignore the 1 for now), that it is a fixed line length file of 80 columns (F 80), and that everything we type will be converted to uppercase (CASE=U). It also says TRUNC= 72 to tell us we can’t go past column 72. This was used for punchcards to identify the card (which is a line in the file).

To navigate, you can use EDIT’s commands up and down. When you follow them with a number, they repeat the operation that number of times. If you are lost, you can use top or bottom to get to the top of the end of the bottom of the file. Let’s do a DOWN 14.

This is how you define VMs. They are called users here, so you might be confused. Some VMs start automatically while others will only be started on LOGON (and will shut down on LOGOFF). This first definitions are, as the comment explains, created to make it easier to define your own users, and you can’t log-on to them yourself.

The file gets more interesting when you look at the ASSIST user. Do a LOCATE ASSIST:

Note: The user ALGOL is also interesting, but we won’t talk about it right now.

Here you see a couple commands before the disk definitions. They tell the user will log-on to the CMS operating environment (IPL means “Initial Program Load” and is what other systems would call “boot”). It’ll then tell how the console is to be used (like an IBM 3215 console as device 009). It’ll also set up a card reader/puncher (one IBM 2540 connected on 00C and 00D) and a printer (an IBM 1403).

Note: The first line tells us the user name (ASSISS), the password (yes, in clear text), and how much memory the machine should have allocated.

Let’s now look into the user we might want to rename. In EDIT, do a LOCATE CMSUSER:

Now you’ll want to open a second x3270 window and log on as CMSUSER.

Notice these are the 3 disks mentioned in the LINK commands after the card reader/puncher and the printer are added. They are being linked from the user MAINT

The first five disks are the ones defined in USER DIRECT in the MDISK lines. The last 3 are the ones LINK’ed from MAIN.

To exit EDIT saving your changes, use the FILE command. To leave without saving changes, use QUIT. You’ll need to clear the terminal to continue (the “MORE…” message in the bottom right). On x3270 PA2, which clears the terminal, is Alt-2.

Another interesting file you might look into. It is a comprehensive list of all disk partitioins, the users those partitions are used by, the CUU they are attached to, and the start and end of the partition, in cylinders. All entries look like this one:

Creating a new user (or VM) would be more work than I’d like to cover here. It’d mean adding new disks, partitioning them, creating filesystems, and adding them to the configuration. Perhaps more importantly, it’d require me to learn a whole lot of things before I could confidently do it.

So, instead of creating a new user, we’ll change the CMSUSER user name and password (passwords can’t have more than 8 characters and are case insensitive). Keep in mind this is how it used to be in the 1970s - modern mainframes are much better protected.

My plan of having a user for me, one for my wife, and users for my kids will have to wait for another day.

TYPE is a fine command (those with CP/M and MS-DOS experience will remember it), but VM/370 CE bundles a full-screen file viewer.

So far we have used EDIT. It’s an editor that comes with IBM’s VM/370 R6 and is the one you might see in period videos, but we have another one that’s much nicer: EE.

Typing EE PROFILE EXEC brings up the editor, in our case, with the PROFILE EXEC file loaded.

EE is a much modern editor, and behaves like current tools, such as ISPF. One nice thing it has is the command are on the left (that region filled with equals signs). Move the cursor up and enter “i3” on one.

When you hit enter, you’ll see EE inserted 3 lines (because that’s what i and 3 mean in this context).

To enter new text (or change what’s on the screen), just move the cursor there and start typing.

We really don’t want that sitting in our PROFILE EXEC file, so we’ll remove those 3 lines.

Now the file is the same as when we started. Pay attention to the purple line at the bottom, and press F3 to quit EE.

Tricked you! F3 will not work because we changed the file (EE doesn’t realize the contents are the same as they are on disk). We’ll use the QQuit command to quit. Think of it as “:q!” on Vim.

Hercules can run without a UI in Shared Device Server mode. In this mode, it can start and no environment needs to be IPL’ed.

Hercules supports routing a printer to a local file. On the packaged version of MVT 3.8j a 1403 line printer is mapped to the prt/prt00f.txt file, and on VM/370 there are two 1403 printers, mapped to io/print1.listing and io/print2.listing. On the container images, these files are not exposed to the host directly, and you’ll have to map the container folder to the host yourself.

Enabling access via a printing terminal (not screen-based).

https://www.vm.ibm.com/download/packages/# https://vmworkshop.org/HENSLER/

4 Action Movie Masterpieces That Define The Entire Genre

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The action genre is an exciting and evolving subsection of cinema that has continued to develop in exciting ways since the dawn of the medium. There’s no clear consensus on when the modern action film began because examples of high-wire setpieces and incredible stuntwork are much older than anything resembling modern blockbusters. The silent films of Buster Keaton and Charlie Chaplin featured remarkable precision and experimentation when it came to actual stuntwork, even if they wouldn’t technically be considered “action stars” by modern pundits. The Western genre, which has been an active part of the industry since the ‘30s, is also where a lot of “action stars” got their start; actors like John Wayne, Steve McQueen, Charlton Heston, Gary Cooper, and Yul Brennar were all accomplished dramatic actors who could do their own fight scenes.

Action cinema began to become more stylized in the 1960s, as the era of World War II adventure films started to sunset in favor of franchises like James Bond, which promised an increased degree of spectacle and theatricality upon each new entry. The ‘70s introduced a darker component to the genre with the rise of vigilante films like Rolling Thunder and the Dirty Harry series, which were more grounded in reality because they drew from contemporary anxieties.

The action genre is often defined by stars, as it seems as if each generation has a few major actors who continuously push the genre forward. Arnold Schwarzenegger and Sylvester Stallone dominated the ‘80s, and their reign continued well into the ‘90s, even if they found new competitors in Tom Cruise and Nicolas Cage. Although today there’s been action stars like Jason Statham and Gerard Butler who have essentially become genres on to themselves, there’s also an appeal in seeing older, more grizzled dramatic actors return to their pulpier roots, including Denzel Washington and Liam Neeson. There are still a few specific action films that have not only stood the test of time, but have a significant degree of influence on pretty much everything that has followed.

Raiders of the Lost Ark was an attempt to do for ‘30s adventure serials what Star Wars had done for science fiction, and producer/creator George Lucas knew that there was only one person who he could trust with bringing the character to life. Steven Spielberg had long since lamented that he was unable to direct a 007 film, as MGM had consistently turned him down because they were not accepting American filmmakers. It was famously when Spielberg and Lucas were on vacation during the opening weekend of Star Wars that they came up with the idea of what the Indiana Jones franchise would look like. Unlike Bond, Indy was a straight-laced professor with a deep knowledge of history and archeology, but he also had a strong desire for justice. Although multiple possibilities were floated for who could possibly take on the role, Harrison Ford eventually became the only real choice, and it’s perhaps the defining part in a career that includes some of the most beloved films of all-time.

Raiders of the Lost Ark is a film that Lucas described as having “just the good parts,” and that is evident within a perfect screenplay by Lawrence Kasdan. There is no fat on the script, as the story moves at a propulsive pace without ever feeling impersonal. Although there are several great installments in the series, Raiders of the Lost Ark remains the best because it has the most exciting MacGuffin, which brings to mind issues of philosophy and faith that add an existentialist level to the film. Many of the action scenes, such as the truck chase and the bi-plane fight, are absolutely brutal in their viscerality, but Spielberg did not forget to also make a film that is equally funny and romantic. The romantic chemistry that Ford has with Karen Allen is nothing short of remarkable, and it’s a major reason why Marion Ravenwood is the love interest that the series has kept coming back to.

Die Hard is a film that has come to represent an entire era of action cinema because of its bullet-proof premise of an ordinary man trapped in a confined location and forced to use his wits and wry wisdom to defend himself and get people to safety. Die Hard was such a massive cultural success that the next generation of action films could all be pitched with their comparisons to Die Hard; Speed was “Die Hard on a bus,” Con Air was “Die Hard on a plane,” Under Siege was “Die Hard on a boat,” and The Rock was “Die Hard in Alcatraz." What distinguished the original Die Hard from all of its imitators was the instantly iconic, brilliant performance by Bruce Willis as John McClane. Although at the time few thought that the guy from Moonlighting would be the next great action star, Willis understood the heart, humanity, and humor needed to play McClane, who somehow felt like a more relatable character than the types of roles that Schwarzenegger and Stallone were playing at the same time.

Die Hard’s genius was understanding that having a great villain was just as important as having a great villain, and cinematic antagonists rarely get better than Alan Rickman as Hans Gruber. Rickman had never acted in a film before, but became perfectly suited to be the cruel, sarcastic, and deviously clever criminal mastermind whose motivations aren’t exactly what they appear to be at first. The dynamic between Willis and Rickman is why Die Hard hits on another level, even if they don’t share that many scenes together. It’s also why none of the Die Hard sequels were able to quite live up to the promise of the first film; the best of the other films in the series is the third installment, Die Hard With A Vengeance, which honored Rickman by introducing Jeremy Irons to play his brother, Simon Gruber.

The Matrix came at a time in which the science fiction genre needed a boost of energy due to it being overrun by maximalist blockbusters and established franchises. The Wachowski sisters made a deeply philosophical warning about the rise of artificial intelligence that also drew from the philosophy of Plato and the type of martial-arts that was traditionally found in East Asian action cinema, as well as strong components of the cyberpunk aesthetic. The result was a groundbreaking achievement in worldbuilding and spectacle that both looked like something that cinephiles had never seen before and got them to question the nature of their reality. Due to the new rules for what its universe looked like, The Matrix was able to reinvent the way that fight scenes were choreographed in the digital age with cutting-edge techniques, such as the now-famous introduction of “bullet time.”

The Matrix is cutting edge as a work of slick, propulsive action filmmaking due to the inclusion of advanced and superhuman fight techniques within a tactical environment, but it is also a classic “hero’s journey” story that benefitted from the charisma of Keanu Reeves. Although Reeves had already proven himself to be a talented action star with Point Break and Speed, The Matrix perfectly cast him to play the avatar for humanity who has to accept his destiny to become “The One.” Although the later installments in The Matrix franchise got a little too wrapped up in their own mythology and strayed away from the emotional sincerity of the original premise, the first film remains a perfect masterpiece that has held up just as well today.

Mad Max: Fury Road was a film that came 30 years in the making, as George Miller had desired to make a fourth installment in the Mad Max franchise ever since he wrapped on the third entry, Mad Max Beyond Thunderdome, in 1985. Although a massive script was written that included an outline for the prequel film that he would end up making, Miller was constantly faced with setbacks and issues regarding funding; after 9/11 delayed shooting and Mel Gibson became too old to play the iconic titular character, it seemed like his dream would never come to fruition.

It was against all odds that Miller ventured into the Australian desert once more on a famously grueling film shoot in which wild rumors suggested that there was a massive disaster in place, and that skepticism didn’t let up until Mad Max: Fury Road debuted at the Cannes Film Festival in 2015 and solidified itself as one of the greatest films of the 21st century, regardless of genre. Mad Max: Fury Road is pure adrenaline and propulsiveness, as it is essentially one long chase. On a deeper level, Mad Max: Fury Road follows a drifter who gets caught up in a revolution, and the launching of Charlize Theron’s Furiosa as one of the defining action heroes of all-time took the great franchise to another level.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Polygon wants to move 70 million POL without the wallet holders’ signatures

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The proposed fork would make ownership proofs, later deposits and exchange accounting central to returning stolen coins.

Polygon's proposed recovery of roughly 70 million POL would move coins out of five hack-linked wallets without their holders signing a transfer. The plan would change balances through the network's rules, putting the authority to return stolen funds in the hands of governance and validators.

The November 4 proposal targets five addresses it links to stolen funds from security incidents affecting prediction market Polymarket and exchange Binance. Its dated reference tables attribute about 24 million POL to four Polymarket-linked addresses and 47 million POL to one Binance-linked address. The actual recovery would use balances at activation, not these dated reference amounts.

As of November 9, the published proposal still leaves the recovery fork's name, client release and activation block undecided. The November 5 governance agenda lists the proposal for discussion without recording approval. The separate Bor v2.11.0 release implements Uburu's StateReceiver changes, rather than this proposed recovery.

Under the proposed design, the upgraded software would read each listed address's balance immediately before activation, set it to zero and credit the same amount to the designated recovery address. This would happen before transactions execute in the activation block. Total supply would stay the same, and earlier chain history would remain intact. The proposal says Bor engineering must still confirm the design.

A single protocol rule would move native POL from five listed accounts to two company-designated recovery addresses. Approval and recipient verification remain conditions.

The rule would read each listed account's full balance from F−1. The recovery credit has not executed.

Before ordinary transactions in F, the five source balances would be zero and the designated recipients credited by the same amounts.

The proposed recovery changes balances at one block, so custody records need a separate adjustment.

Governance approval, recipient-control checks and Bor engineering confirmation are required. The activation block and recovery release remain undecided as of November 8.

Schematic of the published proposed full-balance rule. Account markers show address counts, not relative POL amounts. The panel compares successive steps of the proposed activation sequence; it does not depict observed recovery.

Governance approval, recipient-control checks and Bor engineering confirmation are required. The activation block and recovery release remain undecided as of November 8.

That would place recovery authority in a combination of governance approval, the specified address list and the software rule validators adopt. The affected companies would designate the recipients. Polygon Labs would confirm the proposed proof of recipient control. The proposed sweep would not require a transaction signed by the source addresses.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Those are separate responsibilities. Tracing stolen coins to an address supports the case for including it; proving control of a recovery address checks where the coins would go. The proposal itself warns that misidentifying an attacker-controlled address could move a legitimate balance.

Its recipient-verification requirements call for proof of control, such as a signed message or small test transfer confirmed by Polygon Labs, plus written confirmation that the address belongs to the recipient. The proposal also excludes other tokens and a separate category of blocked addresses, limiting the proposed sweep to native POL at the five listed accounts.

The full-balance rule creates another ownership question: later deposits would move too. The proposal requires balances to be checked again before activation and any changes to be published.

Those checks would disclose which coins the sweep includes, but would not establish that every later deposit belonged to an incident victim.

For exchanges and custodians, the proposed movement would create a difference between the balance shown by an upgraded node and the balance reconstructed solely from ordinary transactions. The proposal explicitly flags this problem for explorers, indexers and accounting tools.

A reconciliation would therefore need to connect each affected source address, its balance before activation, the designated recipient and the corresponding credit at the activation block. For custodians, the rule implies a separate balance adjustment, since there would be no ordinary transfer transaction to match against it.

The planned checks address part of that burden: an Amoy rehearsal would test that supply stays constant and unrelated accounts remain unchanged, followed by a mainnet-fork replay. The proposal also calls for publishing the transaction-free balance change and a verification script after activation. Together, those records would let operators compare the implemented change with the approved scope.

Customer reimbursement is a separate process. In its October 30 postmortem, Polymarket said 95% of affected wallets had been refunded in full that afternoon and announced $4 million being returned, including $1 million for pause-related losses. Those refunds compensate traders; the proposed fork would return assets to company-designated recovery addresses.

The next recovery milestone would require a governance decision, a final software specification, verified recipients and an activation block.

AI Predicts PTSD Risk Based on Stories About What Brought Patients to the Emergency Room

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

NEW YORK, Nov. 9, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- An AI tool can analyze patients' narratives about injuries that sent them to the emergency room to predict who will later develop post-traumatic stress disorder (PTSD), a new study found.

About 43 million patients are admitted to a US emergency department (ED) each year with physical injuries (trauma), and more than a quarter of them develop PTSD, according to past studies. That makes the ED a checkpoint for catching the condition early, but clinicians face time constraints severe enough to rule out the use of standard screening questionnaires.

"PREVIA could open a window for timely care approaches, when preventive intervention is most effective."

Led by NYU Langone Health researchers, the new study found that the team's set of AI models â each analyzing the trauma narratives of 145 ED patients â correctly identified more than 95.4 percent of at-risk patients. In a head-to-head test, the AI tool, called PREVIA, flagged 100 percent of 30 cases from the same patient group that went on to develop PTSD, while an ED clinician specializing in PTSD correctly identified 89 percent of these cases.

"This is the first study to show that a patient's spontaneous answers to a single, routine care question â 'What brought you to the ED?' â carries enough of a psychological signal to forecast their PTSD risk a month later," said senior study author Katharina Schultebraucks, PhD, associate professor in the departments of psychiatry and population health at NYU Langone. "PREVIA can serve as an AI screener that identifies at-risk patients and explains why, without adding any time to intake in busy emergency rooms."

Published online November 9 in Nature Communications, the new work examined the capabilities of generative AI, which predicts options for the next word in a sentence based on how billions of people used that word in context. This sensitivity to language lets the models analyze a person's description of what happened to them to find patterns linked to their risk of developing PTSD, the researchers said. The patients' answers were recorded and transcribed for analysis.

"The emergency department is often the only point of contact a trauma survivor has with the healthcare system before symptoms become much harder to treat," added Dr. Schultebraucks, who is also co-director of computational psychiatry at NYU Grossman School of Medicine. "By identifying at-risk patients at that first encounter, PREVIA could open a window for timely care approaches, when preventive intervention is most effective."

The first month after trauma is a key window for finding those at risk for PTSD because many patients are no longer in contact with the hospital after that, the researchers said. Current questionnaires â such as the Peritraumatic Dissociative Experiences Questionnaire and the Posttraumatic Adjustment Scale â effectively forecast PTSD development, yet only about 28 percent of EDs routinely screen for PTSD at all, and only two-thirds of those use validated tools.

This likely reflects overcrowding and limited clinician time, typically about 15â20 minutes per patient, which forces clinicians to prioritize immediate medical concerns over lengthy mental health assessments, the study authors said.

PREVIA was designed as a pipeline of steps that use different AI tools based on their strengths (e.g., the Llama model tends to emphasize signs of PTSD risk, while Mistral highlights protective and contextual factors that could buffer against it). Specifically, the tool's first layer scans the language of a patient's narrative for symptoms that happen during the initial trauma, like loss of control or amnesia, as well as early symptoms that show up while a person is in the ED, such as feeling disconnected from the body. Both types of symptoms have been linked to later PTSD development.

The second layer of the team's AI tool also weighs risk factors such as pre-existing mental health problems or lower socioeconomic status, alongside protective factors such as coping skills or strong social support. A third AI model synthesizes these assessments into a structured summary, reducing the chance that any single model dominates the results. A final layer combines that summary with demographic information (e.g., age, sex), trauma type, and known PTSD prevalence rates to produce a risk score with a plain-language explanation.

Experiments confirmed that this three-layer design consistently outperformed simpler configurations across most performance metrics. Unlike AI that succeeds or fails for reasons unknown, PREVIA explains its reasoning and grounds predictions in established PTSD risk factors. Results with the tool were also validated by comparing them to diagnoses made for the same cases using Clinician-Administered PTSD Scale for DSM-5 (CAPS-5), the gold standard clinical interview for diagnosing PTSD.

"We built PREVIA on open-source models with no training or fine-tuning required," said Dr. Schultebraucks. "That means other hospitals can test it independently, without access to proprietary software or our patient data, which is essential for widespread adoption."

Along with Dr. Schultebraucks, authors of the study from NYU Langone were Victoria Mueller, Jyoti Yadav, Annie Farnsworth, Charlotte Hilberdink, Sapir Gershov, and Arieh Shalev in the Department of Psychiatry, Eric Oermann in the Department of Neurosurgery, and Stephen Wall in the Ronald O. Perelman Department of Emergency Medicine.

The study was funded by the National Institute for Mental Health grant R01 MH129856 (PI Dr. Katharina Schultebraucks).

About NYU Langone HealthNYU Langone Health is a fully integrated health system that consistently achieves the best patient outcomes through a rigorous focus on quality that has resulted in some of the lowest mortality rates in the nation. Vizient Inc. has ranked NYU Langone No. 1 out of 118 comprehensive academic medical centers across the country four years in a row, and "U.S. News & World Report" recently ranked four of its clinical specialties No. 1 in the nation. NYU Langone offers a comprehensive range of medical services with one high standard of care across seven inpatient locations, its Perlmutter Cancer Center, and more than 330 outpatient locations in the New York area and Florida. The system also includes two tuition-free medical schools, in Manhattan and on Long Island, and a vast research enterprise.

Media Inquiries:Gregory.Williams@NYULangone.org

SOURCE NYU Grossman School of Medicine and NYU Langone Health

Solana Targets Durable Nonces After $270M Drift Hack as Pump.fun Backlash Grows

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Solana developers are preparing to remove durable nonces, the transaction feature exploited in April’s $270 million Drift Protocol hack. The proposed change marks a major shift in how the network handles offline approvals, with implications for exchanges and institutional custodians.

The proposal, known as SIMD-0723, would retire a mechanism that allows signed transactions to remain valid indefinitely. Attackers reportedly exploited this functionality to execute pre-approved transactions more than a week after obtaining signatures from Drift’s security council.

Solana wants to kill the feature behind April's $270M+ Drift hack.SIMD-0723, filed Nov 5 by Anza, would retire "durable nonces": signed transactions that never expire. Normal Solana transactions go stale in ~30 seconds. Earliest switch-off: May 5, 2027. pic.twitter.com/VYYttCsvJk

The move comes as Solana faces another challenge closer to its retail ecosystem. Pump.fun is facing growing community criticism over platform fees, influencer promotions, and allegations of hidden-wallet trading. Together, the developments put renewed attention on security, transparency and trust across Solana’s ecosystem.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

Normal Solana transactions use recent blockhashes that expire after 30 seconds. Durable nonces replace those temporary references with stored values, allowing transactions to remain executable until the nonce advances.

The feature was designed for legitimate operational needs. Exchanges, custodians, and multisig organizations often require offline signing procedures that take longer than Solana’s normal transaction window.

However, attackers turned that convenience into an attack vector against Drift on April 1, 2026. They reportedly secured administrative approvals through air-gapped signing systems before allowing the transactions to sit for more than a week.

Once submitted, the approvals helped attackers take control of the protocol and drain at least $270 million. Drift subsequently attributed the attack to a North Korean-linked group.

Anza contributor Gabe Rodriguez filed SIMD-0723 on November 5. The proposal would require transactions to use recent blockhashes after activation. May 5, 2027, represents the earliest proposed mainnet activation date. The change still requires technical approval and migration planning.

Solana is also exploring replacement programs that could support offline signing without relying on durable nonces. These alternatives would introduce different authorization and transaction-failure considerations for custodians.

The proposal forces institutional users to reconsider how they authorize transactions across Solana. Meanwhile, questions about trust are becoming harder to ignore elsewhere in the ecosystem.

Pump.fun has attracted fresh criticism following several developments involving its business model and influencer ecosystem.

On November 6, blockchain tracker Lookonchain reported that Pump.fun transferred 76,111 SOL, worth approximately $6.85 million, to Kraken. The movement reignited criticism over platform fees and whether its incentives adequately serve ordinary traders.

Pump fun(@Pumpfun) is dumping $SOL again!They just deposited 76,111 $SOL ($6.85M) into #Kraken.In total, https://t.co/TcwYRTArT2 has sold 5,553,833 $SOL($882.4M) at an average price of $158.88.https://t.co/kyztoJLCjt pic.twitter.com/QvzqaR8fGI

Bubblemaps also accused prominent influencers of using undisclosed wallets to purchase tokens before publicly recommending them. Its investigations estimated $125,000 in combined trading profits and promotional rewards involving influencer Ethan.

Separate allegations involving paid streamer collaborations surfaced on November 7. The revelations have intensified questions about transparency within Pump.fun’s promotional ecosystem.

These controversies involve different problems from Drift’s exploit, but they expose a similar challenge: maintaining confidence among participants.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

That challenge also creates an interesting opportunity for Bitcoin-focused projects exploring alternative execution environments.

Bitcoin Hyper is developing a Bitcoin Layer 2 using Solana Virtual Machine technology. Its architecture aims to combine SVM’s high-speed execution with Bitcoin-based settlement, bringing smart contracts, decentralized exchanges, and staking functionality to BTC.

Bitcoin Hyper is not simply another Solana application. Its design uses SVM execution while anchoring Layer 2 state to Bitcoin through periodic commitments and zero-knowledge proofs.

This combination gives it a different proposition from Solana-native applications. Developers can work with an execution environment associated with Solana’s performance, while the project targets Bitcoin’s established settlement infrastructure.

Bitcoin Hyper has also reached several development milestones. Its testnet is live, with a public explorer allowing users to inspect transactions and network activity.

The project’s presale has raised approximately $33.2 million, with HYPER priced at a low $0.0136 per coin. Its November 26 presale deadline leaves 17 days from today for prospective participants to enter before the planned launch window.

The upcoming transition toward mainnet, wallet infrastructure, and exchange listings puts the project in an important stage of development. Its combination of Bitcoin settlement and SVM execution gives it a distinct position in the expanding Bitcoin Layer 2 sector.

BTC price eyes $73K amid Nvidia volatility: Three things to know in Bitcoin this week

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin price action fell to its lowest levels since mid-August as Nvidia reports added to sell-side pressure across stock-market futures.

Bitcoin (BTC) fell to about $73,500 late Sunday, its lowest level since August, as more than $1 billion in leveraged crypto positions were liquidated ahead of this week’s US inflation reports.

Bitcoin slipped below $74,000 shortly before Sunday’s weekly close, extending last week’s losses, TradingView data showed.

BTC/USD one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Crypto liquidations over 24 hours. Source: CoinGlass

BTC/USD one-day chart with 21-week and 50-week SMAs. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Bitcoin also traded below the short-term holder cost basis, which CryptoQuant put at $75,691 on Monday. The metric estimates the average cost basis of coins that last moved less than 155 days ago.

Bitcoin investor cohort cost-basis data. Source: CryptoQuant

A CryptoQuant community analyst put the cost basis for coins that last moved one to three months ago at about $79,300. The analyst said that level could act as resistance if recent buyers sell as they break even.

As markets price in 78.8% odds of the Federal Reserve raising interest rates by 25 basis points in December, per data from CME Group’s FedWatch Tool, multiple Fed officials will take to the stage in the coming days, providing insight on their stance on inflation trends and the economy.

EXCLUSIVE: Trump's FAA touts new AI-powered tool helping thousands of passengers avoid flight delays

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Transportation Secretary Sean Duffy slams Democratic Rep. Don Beyer for calling the FAA's new AI tool an unproven system. Duffy defends the predictive technology on 'Fox & Friends' with Brian Kilmeade, labeling the criticism as complete stupidity. He explains how the tool coordinates schedules to prevent flight delays and safely manage American airspace instead of keeping planes in ground stops.

EXCLUSIVE: The Trump administration's new AI-supported air traffic management tool has already helped reroute nearly 180 flights carrying approximately 24,000 passengers to avoid congestion, cutting flight times by more than 10 minutes on average, according to new Department of Transportation (DOT) data shared exclusively with Fox News Digital.

The Federal Aviation Administration's (FAA) Strategic Management of Airspace, Routes and Trajectories system, known as SMART, has been operational for nearly six weeks, helping air traffic controllers identify more fuel-efficient routes and anticipate potential disruptions.

According to DOT, the technology has also saved nearly 80 gallons of fuel per flight on average, offering an early glimpse of how the Trump administration hopes to reduce flight delays and cancellations.

TRUMP UNVEILS AMERICA.GOV AS AI-POWERED HUB FOR PASSPORTS, MEDICARE ENROLLMENT AND MORE

Transportation Secretary Sean Duffy discusses the Federal Aviation Administration's SMART technology, which uses flight and weather data to help air traffic controllers identify congestion and recommend more efficient routes. (Courtesy Department of Transportation)

"Under President Trump, we are making historic progress to revitalize air traffic control and improve the passenger experience for millions of Americans," Transportation Secretary Sean Duffy said in a statement provided to Fox News Digital.

Duffy also said the technology would deliver broader benefits as the program expands.

"SMART will slash frustrating delays, reduce stress on air traffic controllers, and lower travel prices."

One example came in October when a flight carrying 147 passengers from Ronald Reagan Washington National Airport to Orlando International Airport avoided expected congestion after SMART recommended an alternative route.

SEVERE TURBULENCE DIVERTS NEW YORK CITY FLIGHT TO MIAMI, 8 INJURED

The FAA's Strategic Management of Airspace, Routes and Trajectories system helps air traffic controllers identify more fuel-efficient routes and anticipate potential disruptions. (Stefani Reynolds/Bloomberg via Getty Images, File)

The flight had been scheduled to depart during a period when congestion was expected along its original path. SMART identified a route farther west, which air traffic controllers accepted, shortening the journey by 11 nautical miles and saving six minutes of flight time, according to DOT.

The technology combines 200 data streams, including weather patterns, flight paths, traffic flow and controller staffing metrics, into a centralized platform.

Its AI-supported engine helps aviation specialists anticipate where congestion could develop and recommend more efficient routes before congestion builds.

The technology is also designed to improve how the FAA manages severe weather, a source of disruptions for U.S. air travelers.

PILOTLESS CARGO AIRCRAFT COULD CHANGE HOW PACKAGES MOVE

Aviation specialists work inside the FAA's SMART Center, where technology combines weather, flight path and air traffic data to help identify potential congestion and improve flight efficiency. (Courtesy Department of Transportation)

According to a DOT fact sheet, the agency's existing approach involves blocking off large rectangular sections of airspace affected by storms, even though weather systems are often irregularly shaped and safe corridors may remain available.

SMART is intended to provide a clearer picture of changing weather conditions, allowing aviation specialists to identify routes around storms and determine when restricted airspace can safely reopen.

Despite its AI-supported capabilities, SMART does not independently direct aircraft or replace human air traffic controllers.

A team of aviation specialists analyzes and validates SMART's data before sending scheduling recommendations to the FAA's Air Traffic Control System Command Center. Those recommendations are then forwarded to local air traffic control facilities, where officials decide whether to accept them.

The initiative comes as the nation's busiest airports handle millions of flights annually.

According to DOT, approximately 6.5 million flights operate each year at the nation's 30 busiest airports. About 300,000 receive reroutes before departure, while 3.6 million experience airborne reroutes, vectoring or holding.

The FAA initially limited SMART's rollout to the National Capital Region in September before beginning validation efforts with air route traffic control centers in Miami, Jacksonville and Atlanta in October.

The agency plans to continue testing the system as it works toward expanding SMART to all 22 air traffic control centers nationwide.

While the early results involve a relatively small share of domestic U.S. flights, DOT says the technology is intended to help airlines operate more predictable schedules and ease pressure on air traffic controllers.

For travelers, the Trump administration's goal is fewer delays for passengers, shorter flights and less time lost to flight congestion.
